### 2026-09-03 02:32 EDT V79 delayed-poll and restart exits repaired

- Audited the entry and exit implementation for all nine V79 routes. Stops and
  configured targets resolve from actual fill price with correct long/short
  direction; provider exits are lifecycle-bound; maximum-hold, recipe-exit,
  and profit-lock actions precede and suppress same-cycle entries. SNDK remains
  short-only with an 80-bps stop and 24-hour fallback. Its prior fixed-target,
  trailing, and early-adverse candidates were rejected in development/stress
  evidence, so no speculative exit was installed.
- Reproduced a shared technical defect affecting ETH, SOL, HYPE, ZEC, and SPCX:
  when one poll contained a one-shot recipe exit followed by a later hold row,
  latest-evaluation reduction discarded the exit. Startup readiness did the
  same, and a first post-activation row could bypass the recovery seed entirely.
  A position restored after downtime could therefore miss its exact opposite,
  channel, alignment, or cash-session exit while retaining only its stop and
  maximum-hold fallback.
- Delayed polls now prioritize the latest exit within each provider-symbol
  batch. Startup always runs the separate non-entry recovery scan even when a
  fresh post-activation row exists, retains the latest missed exit, and records
  the exact evaluated scan interval. If a restored recipe-exit lifecycle is
  older than the available scan, it closes fail-closed with
  `provider_recipe_exit_recovery_unverified` instead of pretending the missed
  interval was safe. New entries remain constrained to post-activation rows.
- V79 recovery lookbacks now request at least each honored recipe-exit route's
  terminal lifecycle: ETH/SOL 48 hours, HYPE 24 hours, SPCX 375 minutes, and ZEC
  30 days. Returned-data coverage remains authoritative; the coverage-gap exit
  handles a venue snapshot shorter than the requested interval.
- Red/green regressions first demonstrated all three loss paths: earlier exit
  overwritten by later hold, restart seed choosing the later hold, and first
  fresh row skipping the seed. Controller coverage also proves an old
  unscanned lifecycle closes while a fully scanned no-exit lifecycle remains
  open. The focused poller/controller/runner/V79 set passes 91 tests. The fresh
  full Python suite passes 7,820 tests, with four intentionally deselected and
  27 known dependency/deprecation warnings. Focused Ruff lint/format and diff
  whitespace checks pass.
- Fresh read-only launch resolution still returns profile fingerprint
  `6c12d96e3fe09dd5dcefd24761bc7462ec6d6aa0b0d0f023a21e4b8597b646f7`,
  all seven ordered assets, nine executable routes, the exact V79 accounting
  epoch, and backend/checkpoint/TUI/audit/unsigned-SPCX commands. No live process
  was started or stopped. At 02:32 EDT the latest backend log and checkpoint
  remain stale V78 artifacts from 19:15 EDT, so fresh V79 economic verification
  is still pending the operator launch.

  runtime or TUI strategy.
- The first run stopped before outcomes because the four development caches
  lacked the required seven-day warmup. Immutable 0xArchive Hyperliquid candle
  bundles were added. A common July 27 candle outage was reconstructed from
  complete public-trade bundles (526 minutes per symbol); remaining verified
  no-trade minutes are explicit invalid carry bars. Signal parameters did not
  change.
- No cell qualified for replay. The strongest cell, BTC→SOL at 15 minutes, had
  immediate gross fold means of +0.830/+5.226/+5.987 bps and delayed means of
  +2.414/-0.432/+4.921 bps. Its long/short gross means were +6.160/+1.847 bps,
  both below the 13 bps nominal cost. Immediate medians and trimmed means were
  also nonpositive in two folds.
- The primary family contained 5,861 nominal pair events but only 5,446 unique
  follower/timestamp events after 415 same-follower collisions. The immutable
  sidecar contains 168,798 rows. JSON SHA-256 is
  `5721bf0a11b7616068b2fd5d563cd7415ccd464062ef1cf73a68088faafbd5e4`;
  Parquet SHA-256 is
  `6978a03ca0a29fd0f66fc7e19520feeb80531c0134b46d49ef4e87397b5c72d7`.
- Do not tune neighboring thresholds on the same window. Full contract,
  evidence table, controls, integrity qualification, and falsifier:
  `docs/reviews/2026-08-02-cross-asset-impulse-development.md`.

Confidence is high in rejecting this exact contract on this development
window. A material causality/data correction or a separately frozen mechanism
with stable delayed returns above measured cost would update the conclusion;
post-hoc threshold variants would not.

### 2026-08-02 complete cohort operator cockpit

- The shared BTC/ETH/SOL/HYPE TUI is now an eight-screen cockpit: Portfolio,

  synthesizing missing levels. Chart retains four real completed-1m candlestick

  requests without disabling the automatic portfolio strategies. A manual
  close suppresses only same-cycle re-entry; the next polling cycle resumes

  a short-lived run/profile-bound request only after a confirmation step. The

  position state, isolated sizing, stop protection, and venue constraints, then
  records received/accepted/rejected/completed lifecycle events. Observer mode
  rejects execution requests and the UI disables them.
- Config is a complete next-run profile editor for maximum positions, polling,
  per-coin leverage/margin/notional/stop/hold, and provider/coin execution
  scope. It compares effective and draft values, masks credential presence,
  refuses staging while live is armed, atomically requests backend validation,
  and writes a separate restart-only staged profile. It cannot hot-mutate or
  arm the running backend; live arming remains CLI-only with both explicit risk
  flags.
- Current disarmed observer `bidirectional-observe-1785682136700` was restarted
  on this code. Direct real-ledger inspection observed 39 polls, 62 evaluations,
  all ten provider/coin pairs, zero routed actions, only `live_armed=false` and

  pass rendered current venue prices, spreads, OPI, five depth rows, observer
  evidence, control disablement, and the effective restart profile.
- Journal preserves the backend evidence boundary: `SIGNAL` paths contain only
  eligible fired evaluations, while `SETUP` paths contain readiness-only

  censored, and missing counts for all four horizons. Setup paths cannot be
  presented as fills, PnL, or substitute signal evidence.
- The observer audit now emits a backend-owned ten-mapping strategy scorecard

  all rejected. Journal shows the forward assessment and its inspector shows

  Older checkpoints remain readable and show `NOT REPORTED BY THIS RUN`.
- A one-shot checkpoint of still-running disarmed run
  `bidirectional-observe-1785682136700` at 2026-08-02 20:28 UTC covered all ten
  mappings. Nine had no eligible forward signal. HYPE breakout-onset had one
  short signal with adverse 5/15/30/60-minute directional paths of
  -49.60/-35.18/-13.84/-34.61 bps. This n=1 path has no fill, cost, PnL, edge,

- Hulk, SOL shadow/live, and Apex MM supervisors now capture the existing
  append boundary and refuse to open the TUI until the child publishes a new
  run ID with the exact frozen profile fingerprint and requested arm state.
  A surviving process plus stale ledger data is no longer treated as readiness.
- The shared startup parser fails on prior ledger corruption, truncation,
  invalid UTF-8/JSON, wrong profile, wrong authority, child exit, or timeout.
  An incomplete trailing append is retried until complete rather than accepted.
- SOL shadow and live now have distinct STOP paths. The observer retains
  `data/research/forward/sol_range_onset_canary/STOP`; live owns
  `data/live/sol_range_onset_canary/STOP`. Starting shadow can no longer unlink
  an active live cleanup request.
- Directional shutdown semantics were corrected in operator documentation:
  quitting Hulk or SOL while an exact venue-native bracket is open preserves
  that protected lifecycle for restart reconciliation; it does not force-close
  solely because the TUI exited. Apex MM still cancels quotes, flattens residual
  inventory, and proves flat because it has no directional bracket contract.
- No live flags were supplied and no execution client was constructed. All six
  backend-only/unified live commands were invoked disarmed and refused before
  authority construction.
- Verification: 2,592 offline Python tests passed with four network tests
  deselected; the 524-test candidate lifecycle cohort passed; all 83 Rust/TUI
  tests, strict Clippy, Rust formatting, task-scoped Ruff/formatting, status,
  and whitespace checks passed. The shared startup module has 100% statement
  and branch coverage. Status remains `live.can_start=false` with live disabled
  by default.

Confidence is high in the tested stale-ledger and STOP-file isolation behavior;
the shared startup module has 100% branch coverage and the focused candidate
lifecycle suite passes. A TUI opening without a fresh matching identity, a
shadow process consuming the live STOP file, or a backend/TUI arm mismatch
would falsify readiness.

### 2026-08-01 exact Hyperliquid fill identity

- Hyperliquid user fills now require and preserve the venue `tid`. Missing,
  non-integer, non-positive, or duplicate trade identities fail closed instead
  of falling back to order ID, timestamp, transaction hash, or row count.
- Apex streaming reconciliation deduplicates by `tid`, so distinct partial fills
  sharing an order, timestamp, or transaction hash remain distinct.
- Hulk, SOL range-onset/source-follow, and Apex telemetry persist `trade_id` on
  fill, fill-fee, slippage, and markout rows. Restart baselines and pending
  markouts also use the exact venue identity.
- Hulk and Apex stopped-run auditors compare exact `(tid, oid, exchange time)`
  identities. Equal metric counts for the wrong partial fill are incomplete
  evidence rather than a false pass.
- Fee-schedule rows are now stamped `source=venue_fee_schedule`; restart
  deduplication considers only `source=fill` fee rows. This preserves old
  schedule rows without inventing a trade identity for them.
- The Apex audit coverage logic was extracted into a 299-line single-purpose
  module; the primary audit is 478 lines. Task-scoped Ruff and formatting pass.
- Verification: 2,579 offline Python tests passed with four network tests
  deselected; 83 Rust/TUI tests, strict Clippy, Rust formatting, backend status,
  and `git diff --check` passed. Targeted coverage is 97%, including 100% for
  the exact fill-identity module. Repository-wide Ruff still reports the same
  33 older findings and broad formatting debt outside this repair.
- No strategy parameters, portfolio authority, or arm state changed. Backend
  status remains fail-closed with `live.can_start=false`; a live canary still
  requires fresh explicit approval, both risk flags, credentials, and preflight.

Confidence is high that the tested accounting paths no longer collapse two
venue fills solely because they share order/time/hash fields. A real mainnet
payload lacking `tid`, an exchange-side identity reuse, an unattributed fill,
or any fee/slippage/markout mismatch in the frozen canary audit would falsify
readiness and must halt new entries.

### 2026-08-01 Hulk live-evidence integrity and unified launcher

- `scripts/hulk_smash_canary_tui.py` now supervises the Hulk backend and its
  read-only TUI in one terminal. Observe remains the default and loads only the
  public wallet identity; live requires `--mode live --enabled
  --i-accept-live-risk` plus the existing credential, preflight, and account-lock
  checks. No live process was armed by this change.
- Startup requires a new run ID with the exact frozen profile fingerprint and
  expected arm state. An older process appending to the same ledger cannot fake
  readiness. A backend that dies nonzero is reported rather than treated as a
  clean TUI exit.
- TUI exit, Ctrl-C, and SIGTERM request verified STOP-file cleanup. A live
  backend that cannot complete within 120 seconds is deliberately left running
  with the STOP request intact; the supervisor does not kill a potentially open
  venue lifecycle.
- Hulk execution telemetry now records actual and target markout horizons,
  lateness, and restart provenance. The TUI labels a recovered 7.3-second
  measurement as 7.3 seconds rather than the configured five-second target.
- If both bracket OIDs fill, lifecycle accounting reports
  `mixed_bracket_exit`. It selects only a uniquely latest terminal OID; an exact
  exchange-timestamp tie remains `null` instead of being guessed as target.
- `scripts/hulk_smash_live_canary_audit.py` now snapshots and hashes an exact
  stopped live-ledger prefix with no execution client. It selects only an armed
  mainnet Hulk run, refuses overwrite, preserves zero-fill/losing evidence, and
  verifies exact fill identities for fees, slippage, markouts, exit attribution,
  liquidation-distance coverage, faults, and final flat/open-order truth.
- Focused evidence is green: 154 Hulk/Hyperliquid/runtime-lock Python tests,
  the complete Rust TUI suite, and strict all-target Clippy. The later exact-fill
  section records the completed repository-wide offline verification gate.
- Confidence is medium-high in the tested implementation paths and low in live
  execution quality until an explicitly authorized canary supplies real fills.
  Stale identity acceptance, wrong displayed horizon, invented exit reason,
  non-flat cleanup, or ledger/TUI mismatch would falsify this assessment.

### 2026-07-30 corrected gate robustness and shared-direction closure

- A corrected four-lane session factorial applied every range rule to completed
  five-minute candles and replayed 12,202,083 canonical L2 snapshots plus
  36,931,672 public trades. All lanes retained 35% isolated margin, 30x, an 8%
  gross-margin target, `[06:00,15:00)` UTC entries, and ML observe-only.
- The frozen confidence-0.70/range-12 lead ended at $28,511.41 with 26/26
  targets, no liquidation, and -12.22% maximum drawdown. Removing only the
  range floor ended at $35,671.87 with 35/35 targets, no liquidation, and the
  same drawdown. Removing confidence produced one 24-hour loss and ended at
  $25,978.84; removing both ended at $33,322.53 with the same low-confidence
  loss. Confidence relaxation is rejected.
- The range-only challenger was tested on the older gap-safe continuous path.
  The 12-bps control ended at $24,300.69, PF 2.372, with two full/seven partial
  liquidations. Zero bps ended lower at $22,315.35, PF 1.504, with five
  full/thirteen partial liquidations and one time exit. In the intervening
  ten-day slice zero bps ended at $15,597.18 versus $15,221.67, but had only
  two fills and -9.02% versus -2.27% drawdown.
- Range-floor dominance is therefore non-stationary: 12 bps wins older tail
  quality and equity, while zero bps wins the April and recent return paths.
  Keep 12 bps as the unchanged active control and retain zero bps as a
  report-only forward observer. Do not retune the running process to the best
  recent result.
- Relaxing the range floor adds one visible-creator short timestamp match
  within ten minutes, but its maker order expires. The only matched filled
  short remains the existing July 24 event, so creator cadence fidelity does
  not materially improve.
- The corrected shared portfolio combined the frozen liquidity-sweep long with
  the session short under one $15,000 account and one BTC position. Short-only
  ended at $28,511.41 with -12.22% drawdown. Long-only ended at $15,376.31,
  PF 1.323, with four targets and one stop. Combined ended at $28,521.80 with
  29 targets and one long stop, but drawdown worsened to -18.23%.
- The combined arm adds only $10.38 while worsening drawdown by 6.01 percentage
  points. It reproduced all 26 short fills; the compounded long stop consumed
  nearly all long contribution. Keep the long annotation-only and do not
  promote a two-sided runtime.

### 2026-07-30 35% source-follow shadow cutover

- A live/offline parity audit exposed two distinct data-lineage effects. The
  recorder-backed 10:35 bar was partial across the backend cutover, and the
  startup warm-up had admitted the still-forming 10:40 REST candle. The latter
  was a causal-state bug: a partial warm candle could seed the classifier and
  then be skipped by the live poller's strictly-new timestamp filter.
- The runner now filters REST warm-up input at the activation boundary and
  seeds only candles whose close time is causally available. Tests cover the
  exact boundary. The same candidate was confirmed flat, stopped gracefully,
  and restarted without resetting its ledger or $5,401.15 carried equity. At
  11:05:49 UTC startup, 5,103 of 5,104 warm bars were retained and the forming
  11:05 candle was excluded.
- A new report-only parity command compares backend regime observations with
  checksum-bound offline labels and either DuckDB trade bars or exact REST
  candle evidence. It has no execution authority and fails on mismatches,
  missing evidence, `submitted=true`, or `place_attempted=true`.
- Post-fix exact REST parity passed 4/4 observations from 11:06 through 11:09
  UTC: label time, primary state, confidence, completed range, 60-second pulse,
  and no-submit fields all matched with zero uncovered rows. The candidate
  remained flat in `TREND_UP`. This validates implementation consistency over
  a short window, not profitability or fill behavior.

### 2026-07-27 BBWP tactical-evidence contract

- The faithful Hypervelocity classifier now uses EMA 21/55, a 240-bar
  structure lookback, a 1 bp minimum trend gap, BBWP(20) ranked over 240 bars
  with compression below 25%, 8 bp breakout clearance, and 2.5x the preceding
  20-bar mean volume. Canonical Hypervelocity no longer consumes Choppiness
  Index fields.
- RSI(14) crossing its EMA(7), prior-bar BBWP compression, signed EMA gap,
  breakout direction, volume ratio, and causal range boundaries are published
  as backend-authored tactical evidence. The TUI only projects these fields.
- Entry eligibility remains the authoritative 5m trend direction or a
  direction-confirmed 1m+5m breakout. The 15m context remains non-veto, and
  BBWP/RSI tactical alignment is explicitly not an execution gate.
- This policy choice is empirical rather than assumed: across 129,600 strict
  Hyperliquid 1m bars, the full tactical conjunction emitted 13 annotations
  and matched 0/34 reconciled recorded entries, including zero within 30
  minutes. Structural eligibility matched 23/34 exactly and 26/34 within 10
  minutes.
- Relative to the prior trial-60 configuration, the pre-reconciliation exact
  direction comparison changed from 25/35 to 24/35. The corrected current
  result is 23/34, with two false-RANGE entries and five no-entry states.
  Full-window churn changed from 1,811 to 1,534, and mean transition latency
  from 7.54 to 9.93 bars. This is not evidence of improved profitability; fresh
  venue-matched shadow logs can falsify or update the selection.
- The requested 5m wick-to-close proximity rule is not executable because no
  numeric zone construction or distance tolerance was supplied. Causal range
  boundaries are exposed for measurement without inventing a new gate.
- The structural report is
  `data/logs/research/hypervelocity_regime_eval_bbwp_structural_20260727.json`;
  the strict-gate falsification report is
  `data/logs/research/hypervelocity_regime_eval_bbwp_tactical_20260727.json`.
- Verification passed 758 offline Python tests with four network tests
  deselected, all 33 Rust TUI integration tests, strict Clippy, Rust formatting,
  changed-file Ruff, and `git diff --check`. Whole-repository Ruff still
  reports 33 unrelated pre-existing violations outside this change.

### 2026-07-25 implementation status

- TUI projection now recognizes canonical `TREND_UP`/`TREND_DOWN`/`BREAKOUT`/`TRANSITION` labels and uses live backend `regime_observation` history for the distribution bar. It falls back to session identity for 40x leverage and reports net trade PnL—including entry/exit fees and funding—as a percentage of actual allocated margin.
- Normal TUI/backend quit no longer resets shadow state. The backend resumes the latest filled Hypervelocity run from its ledger, including initial cash, realized PnL, open position, and last exit cooldown; only the explicit backed-up reset command starts a fresh portfolio.
- Closed journal trades are selectable and expose Eastern entry/exit times, entry/exit 1m/5m/15m regime context, entry/exit fees, funding, gross PnL, and net PnL. The active-position box reports paid entry fee, accrued funding, and estimated maker exit fee.
- The dashboard now allocates enough height to display five backend-published bid levels and five ask levels. Labels match the implemented composite: authoritative 5m primary, direction-confirmed 1m+5m breakouts, and 15m non-veto context. A 1m trend cannot override 5m `RANGE`, `TRANSITION`, or `UNKNOWN`.
- `hypervelocity-shadow-reset` is dry-run by default. `--yes` obtains the backend singleton lock, creates a timestamped audit backup, and clears only shadow decisions and the backend text log; cache, regime snapshots, models, and configuration remain intact.
- `hypervelocity-tui` is the single operator entry point: Python supervises the no-submit backend, waits for fresh ledger telemetry, runs the Rust TUI in the foreground, and terminates/reaps the backend child when the TUI exits. Backend logs are isolated from the terminal screen under `<data-root>/paper/hypervelocity-shadow.log`.
- A per-data-root advisory lock refuses a second Hypervelocity backend, preventing two independent BTC positions or competing latest-run telemetry.
- `hypervelocity-shadow-run --config configs/hypervelocity_faithful.toml` starts one bidirectional backend. It permits one signed BTC position, cannot reverse while open, and may switch sides only after the 8% margin target closes the prior trade.
- Startup validates current public Hyperliquid BTC metadata. On 2026-07-25 the live response reported `szDecimals=5`, `maxLeverage=40`, and hourly funding `0.0000125`.
- Tier-0 fees are explicit: maker `0.00015`, taker `0.00045`. Qualified post-only fills use maker fees and no invented slippage; funding refreshes every 60 seconds and applies only at hourly settlement boundaries while a position is open.
- Hyperliquid price ticks are calculated from venue precision rules rather than a permanently fixed `$1` assumption. At the observed BTC price near `$64k`, the effective tick is `$1`.
- Verification: 439 offline Python tests passed, 20 Rust TUI integration tests passed, targeted Ruff passed, the TUI binary built, TOML parsed, and the prior real PTY launch/quit smoke wrote unified no-submit telemetry and reaped the backend child.
- The unified shadow state was reset on 2026-07-25 after the first observation window. The prior decisions and backend log are recoverable under `data/ops/hypervelocity_visual/backups/shadow_reset_20260725T141543Z`; active decisions/log files were verified at zero bytes while cache and regime-snapshot checksums remained unchanged.

- `paper-run --metalabel-mode observe` records model probability and bundle identity without changing conviction or entry eligibility.
- Every backend run stamps its run/export/strategy/execution/model identity and effective strategy, exit, risk, and cost configuration in `session_start`.
- The TUI reads `data/paper/decisions/decisions.jsonl`, follows the persistent filled Hypervelocity run across supervised restarts, and projects only backend-confirmed trades, position, PnL, equity, and model annotations.
- Missing or invalid telemetry is shown as disconnected/stale/error; the TUI does not synthesize replacement trades.
- Research, sweep, backtest, autotune, and model-training CLI commands remain separate from both the backend daemon and TUI process.
- Candidate bundles are retained under `data/models/metalabel/candidates/`; neither 2026-07-25 Hypervelocity candidate was installed because chronological OOS log loss did not beat the constant-probability baseline.
- Paper cost resolution now accepts the canonical exported `funding_rate_1h` when the legacy `funding_rate_8h` field is null; this was validated with the real Hypervelocity export.

### 2026-07-25 faithful-contract validation

The recorded strategy facts supplied by Albert supersede the speculative
32%-risk, ATR-stop, and split-exit hypotheses in `docs/external`. The backend
now enforces the observed baseline:

- BTC 1m only, with separate long and short strategy IDs.
- Entries only during canonical `TREND_UP`, `TREND_DOWN`, or a direction-confirmed real breakout.
- `RANGE`, `TRANSITION`, `UNKNOWN`, and directionless breakout create no entry.
- 70% of current equity is margin at 40x (28x-equity notional).
- One 8% return-on-margin target (0.2% BTC price move), with no invented stop or split exit.
- Post-only entry and target orders. Streaming paper execution retains pending orders and requires one-tick-through evidence; missing L2 falls back to a causal next-completed-bar through test.
- LightGBM and temporal/logistic candidates are annotations only. CLI requests to scale or filter Hypervelocity are converted to `observe`, and the effective policy is stamped in the backend ledger.
- TUI trade, position, PnL, equity, risk, exit, model, and order-execution fields come from the backend ledger. Display fallback values are fixed-contract labels only and never execute trades.

Corrected causal regime evaluation (candle-open timestamps become available one
minute later) used 35 unique recorded entry decisions:

| Candidate | Train exact | Holdout exact | Total exact | RANGE/no-entry at logged entries |
|-----------|------------:|--------------:|------------:|---------------------------------:|
| Scalar-score winner (trial 91) | 21/24 | 9/11 | 30/35 (85.7%) | 1 |
| Selected zero-false-range candidate (trial 248) | 19/24 | 10/11 | 29/35 (82.9%) | 0 |

Confidence is high for those counts on the evaluated cache and medium for
general range accuracy: the manual log contains positive entries, not labeled
negative/range intervals, and 11.9% of the evaluated candles came from
Hyperliquid while the remainder came from Binance rather than the original
recording venue. A point-in-time venue-matched labeled range set would update
this conclusion.

The bounded order-book replay used 32,609 BTC L2 snapshots and produced 8
filled entries, 7 completed trades, one open position, and one expired entry
from a $71.23 flat-start account; final equity was about $98.64 after 1.5 bp
maker fees. Touch and one-tick-through bounds agreed on those fills, but queue
position, cancellations, partial fills, and the already-open real position at
the beginning of coverage remain unknown. Therefore exact timestamp-for-
timestamp strategy replication is not yet proven.

The faithful replay annotation comparison used 4,219 overlapping rows and four
purged/embargoed chronological folds:

| Model | OOS log loss | OOS Brier | Decision |
|-------|-------------:|----------:|----------|
| Constant past-only baseline | 0.13747 | — | Reference |
| LightGBM | 0.28282 | 0.07958 | Retain only; did not beat baseline |
| Current-row logistic | 0.68939 | 0.24199 | Retain only; did not beat baseline |
| Three-event lag logistic | 0.70004 | 0.23935 | Retain only; did not beat baseline |

A TCN remains a future research option, not a missing runtime dependency:
PyTorch/TensorFlow are absent and the effective independent sample count is
materially lower than 4,219 because windows overlap. It should be tested only
after venue-matched labels and replay fidelity are stronger.

Verification evidence:

- `cargo test -p hypervector-tui`: 8 passed.
- `uv run pytest tests/ -q -m "not network" -k "not ray_adapter_executes_through_vectorbt"`: 400 passed, 5 deselected.
- The excluded pre-existing VectorBT/Ray adapter test terminated its worker process before producing a result in this host environment; it is unverified, not counted as passed.
- All changed Python files pass targeted Ruff. Repository-wide Ruff still reports 37 pre-existing issues in unrelated modules.
- `status` and `paper-check` passed: shadow is no-submit, live remains unarmed/fail-closed, and no secret values were printed.
- Both TOML profiles parse successfully.
- A bounded 500-bar shadow run on cached BTC 1m data produced two pending entries, two causal maker entry fills, one fixed-target close, and zero submit attempts. Its ledger stamped 70% margin, 28x notional, 40x/8%, no stop, post-only, and one-tick-through execution.

Candidate evidence:

| Side | Labels | WF folds | OOS log loss | Baseline | OOS AUC | Decision |
|------|-------:|---------:|-------------:|---------:|--------:|----------|
| Long | 11,193 | 9 | 0.6574 | 0.6344 | 0.5311 | Retain, do not promote |
| Short | 12,928 | 9 | 0.6457 | 0.6297 | 0.5237 | Retain, do not promote |

The current label builder samples true entry bars and applies the export's generic barrier exits. It is not yet empirically proven to match every native backend trade lifecycle; that audit is required before model promotion.

### 2026-07-25 venue-matched shadow reconciliation pilot

The backend now supports a report-only
`hypervelocity-shadow-compare` command. It selects explicit shadow run IDs,
defines the evidence window from live `regime_observation` rows, matches
same-side entries one-to-one within a reported tolerance, and reports timing,
direction, RANGE-at-real-entry, maker submit-to-fill, exit, and price
discrepancies. A non-overlapping real log fails explicitly instead of
returning a misleading zero-match result.

Live daemon warm-up is now observation-only: historical bars seed the rolling
indicator buffer but cannot create historical shadow orders or alter starting
equity. Offline replay behavior remains unchanged. Capped real REST sessions
also wait for completed bars; only injected test/replay feeds use the
three-empty-poll exhaustion rule.

First isolated pilot root:
`data/experiments/shadow_20260725T1324Z`.

- Warm history: 5,193 BTC 1m bars, 100% Hyperliquid provenance, zero detected
  gaps, ending 2026-07-25 13:24 UTC.
- Long run `3904919d8b03` and short run `e2f4043b6594`: five live observations
  each from 13:26 through 13:30 UTC, zero fills, zero venue submit/place
  attempts, clean `max_live_bars` stop.
- All ten observations were `TREND_UP`; long eligibility was 5/5 and short
  eligibility was 0/5. The long lane created one post-only `submit_entry`, but
  no one-tick-through fill occurred before the window ended.
- L2 recorder captured 71 Hyperliquid snapshots from 13:24:42 through
  13:27:54 UTC, then stopped on HTTP 429. Maker evidence is therefore partial,
  not a complete-window validation.
- The available manual real-strategy log ends at 2026-07-24 15:06 UTC. The new
  comparator correctly refused it because it does not overlap the July 25
  shadow window. A same-window real log remains required for a match score.

For the next paired collection, use a slower REST cadence (for example 10
seconds for the two shadow daemons and 5 seconds for L2 recording) to reduce
HTTP 429 risk. Rate limits, zero trades, performance, and mismatch counts remain
reported observations rather than implicit gates.

### 2026-07-27 90-day Hypervelocity parameter comparison

The complete report-only comparison is retained at
`data/logs/research/hypervelocity_archive_parameter_comparison_20260727.json`.
It verified 2,160 hourly bundles, processed 129,600 strict venue bars,
12,013,542 L2 snapshots, and 36,591,762 deduplicated public trades. All 12
baseline/single/pair/full candidates were screened; the original Pareto60,
full BBWP blueprint, EMA21/55-only, and EMA21/55+8 bps clearance candidates
received full touch/through/trade-through replay.

| Candidate | L2 final-equity range | Liquidations | Maximum drawdown range |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Pareto60 original | $0.99-$3.79 | 43-47 | -99.995% to -99.988% |
| Full BBWP blueprint | $2.71-$6.54 | 41-43 | -99.985% to -99.965% |
| EMA21/55 only | $2.71-$8.84 | 40-43 | -99.985% to -99.952% |
| EMA21/55 + 8 bps clearance | $2.84-$6.85 | 41-43 | -99.984% to -99.963% |

Observed target rates were 85.03%-85.92%, but 70% isolated margin at 40x made
the repeated liquidation losses dominate the 8%-of-margin targets. The full
BBWP configuration did not win: EMA-only retained the most equity under public
trade-through, while EMA+clearance narrowly led touch/through. Changing only
Choppiness85 to BBWP25 produced the same entry/structural decision fingerprint
as the original on this window; it added no measured decision value.

The replay now deducts maker entry/target fees, taker liquidation fees, applies
the configured partial-liquidation threshold/cooldown, marks drawdown, enforces
same-direction structural reset, and holds open positions through RANGE. It
uses book midpoint as a historical mark-price proxy and BBO liquidation fills
without depth. Historical funding, true queue rank, venue mark price, and
depth-aware liquidation execution remain unavailable, so exact balances have
medium confidence. The conclusion that every tested 70%/40x TP-only path has
catastrophic modeled drawdown has high confidence across the tested
assumptions. No candidate was promoted and runtime behavior was not changed.

Next option study, not an approved runtime change: compare the unchanged
TP-only control with causal prior-consolidation wick, confirmed pivot,
buffered wick/pivot, and liquidation-proximity emergency stops. Stop fills must
use taker fees and adverse gap/BBO execution, and report stopped trades that
would later have reached the original target. Design:
`docs/plans/2026-07-27-hypervelocity-emergency-stop-options.md`.

### 2026-07-27 creator evidence and loss-control closure

Two creator screenshot rows are normalized at
`docs/external/creator_screenshot_positions_20260727.csv`. The prior $63,893
short is upgraded from tracker-only to a visible +8% outcome, and a new July 25
$64,395 long visibly exits at $64,523 for +8%. The outcome-backed source set is
now 23 logical entries. Completed-bar 5m primary structure matches 20/23
directions, including both new rows; executable `entry_direction` matches
17/23 because three additional matching breakouts still await 1m confirmation.
The three structural misses are successful early reversals that precede the
current 5m direction.

The creator-prescribed 23-entry schedule produced 19 maker fills and 19 targets
on the same July source window, moving $120 to $290.05 with -34.30% maximum
drawdown. This confirms that the source timing can work under the replayed
venue contract, but it is in-sample and does not reveal the trigger. The
canonical 90-day pressure replay still produced 261 targets and 41
liquidations across 302 fills, reducing $120 to $0.0668.

No tested loss-control candidate qualified for runtime promotion. A 50th-
percentile counter-context strength delay worsened liquidations and equity. A
strict causally confirmed pivot avoided 37 liquidations but stopped 117
eventual targets and still lost about 99.33% of fixed-lifecycle equity. Fixed
stops, consolidation-wick stops, liquidation-proximity stops, entry freshness,
and first-opposing-1m loss exits were also rejected. Exact artifacts and
evidence limitations are recorded in
`docs/reviews/2026-07-27-creator-screenshot-forward-validation.md`.

The original reverse-engineering plan contains unsupported hypotheses about a
tight/trailing stop, 32% allocation, split positions, 15m hard veto, funding
reversal, and dynamic leverage. They are not runtime requirements. The explicit
contract remains 70% margin, 40x isolated, one BTC position, 8% fixed margin
target, maker-only execution, no RANGE entries, hold through RANGE, 5m primary,
15m non-veto context, and observe-only ML. Configuration SHA-256 remains
`801d2c7af3c1211c13fea49dda673fdb8ed40ff0909692e9cebad070c0e3246f`.

### 2026-07-27 creator-calibrated fixed-stop grid

The predeclared no-stop/6%/10%/14%/18% gross-margin grid was replayed over the
unchanged faithful detector with verified public trade-through fills,
authentic mark/oracle prices, and official funding timestamps. All five paths
lost more than 99.9% from the $10,000 shadow balance and every 30-day slice was
negative. The 14% stop was numerically least bad at $6.31 final equity, versus
$3.80 for the no-stop control, but is not viable. Fixed stops reduced modeled
liquidations from 40 to one while creating 131-234 stops and more re-entry
churn. No runtime or TUI strategy behavior changed.

`data/logs/research/hypervelocity_creator_margin_stop_grid_20260727.json`,
SHA-256
`b0e6c9d584db7ffd631da810cdca7ca656025c07af681a36345843e800884b37`.

### 2026-07-27 stop-14% outcome-metalabel closure

The faithful 14% stop replay supplied a less-sparse observe-only label: 371
resolved fills contained 227 targets, 143 stops, and one liquidation. Three
outcome-aware purged folds yielded 186 OOS predictions and 75 losses. The
past-only base rate won mean log loss at 0.67861. Bar/regime logistic reached
0.69819 and mean ROC AUC 0.5047; bar/regime LightGBM reached 0.72168 and AUC
0.4273. Adding venue L2 worsened mean log loss in both model families.

The strongest learned economic slice was logistic without L2: its fold-local
lowest-risk halves averaged 65.59% targets, below the fee-adjusted 70.69%
break-even rate, and the first fold reached only 51.61%. No candidate exceeded
break-even in all three folds. ML therefore remains `observe` with
`may_filter=false`, `may_scale=false`, and no installed model. Runtime strategy
and faithful configuration were unchanged.

Artifact:
`data/logs/research/hypervelocity_stop14_outcome_metalabel_20260727.json`,
SHA-256
`bd4e36c0ac7e9f4aa5c51113a8bbc816d54512f53454fefe1399b5aa7a239f64`.
The evidence rejects these six frozen challengers on this development window;
it does not establish the creator's actual losing-trade rule. New creator
screenshots should continue to be reconciled in `docs/external`, with losing
fills and exit reasons carrying the highest information value.

### 2026-07-27 source-bounded maximum-hold closure

The frozen no-timeout/6h/12h/24h comparison used the unchanged faithful
detector and complete verified 90-day event tape. Control finished at $3.80
from $10,000. The 6h, 12h, and 24h candidates finished at $0.80, $18.17, and
$4.47 respectively; every candidate lost money in every 30-day slice and all
maximum drawdowns exceeded 99.92%. Twelve hours was numerically least bad but

### 2026-07-28 DuckDB research infrastructure

Historical replay no longer requires every experiment to parse thousands of
hourly gzip files. A local DuckDB analytical store now ingests checksum-verified
0xArchive bundles in 24-hour transactions, retains complete 20-level books and
public trades, materializes causal microstructure features, fingerprints source
manifests for idempotency, and fails explicitly on tampering or count mismatch.
Paper/live execution and strategy authority are unchanged.

The complete available BTC window, 2026-04-28 through 2026-07-28 (91 days), is
materialized at `data/research/market.duckdb`: 2,184 hourly pairs, 12,202,084
source L2 rows, and 36,931,690 source public trades. Vendor responses include
1,026 unique books at hourly inclusive ends and 18 economically identical trade
IDs repeated across adjacent requests. Canonical replay therefore retains all
unique L2 rows, deduplicates only the 18 public trades to 36,931,672, and
applies half-open filtering at the requested outer window. The bounded 91-day
stream contains 12,202,083 books because one observation equals the final end.

The build took 160.59 seconds, produced a 3.6 GiB database, and peaked at
11.88 GiB RSS. A full canonical 91-day one-minute feature aggregation took
0.409 seconds; converting the full canonical window into 91 bounded pandas
chunks took 8.07 seconds and peaked at 1.92 GiB RSS. These are host-specific
measurements, not general performance guarantees. Ten focused infrastructure
tests cover idempotency, full depth/features, checksum rejection, transactional
rollback, inclusive-end normalization, CLI lifecycle, and data layout.

An exact strict Recipe C short-follow parity replay then matched every prior
submission, fill, trade, lifecycle, funding cashflow, liquidation, equity, and
drawdown value. Development counted one fewer snapshot only because the bounded
query excludes the observation exactly at its outer end. The accepted parity
artifact SHA-256 is
`2a66e3d72336ea1e9f2262bcc30bb5a9760ff741410e290ca549f496322efba2`.

  profit. It is an experiment target, not a guaranteed income claim.

## Evidence-tiered cohort and BTC L2 return challenger — 2026-08-02

- The current BTC 5m L2 target-hit classifier was rescreened at 13 bps total
  friction. LightGBM thresholds that fired lost heavily; thresholds at or above
  0.45 emitted no trades because the maximum OOS probability was 0.449.
- A separate development-only Ridge/LightGBM expected-gross-return challenger
  retained the frozen 25/18-bps barrier and 60-minute horizon. Corrected V2
  produced mean rank IC 0.0355 for Ridge and 0.0227 for LightGBM. The largest
  fold quintile spread was 3.25 bps versus the 26-bps feasibility requirement
  for 13-bps round-trip friction. Both models predicted less than 13 bps on
  every row, so no strategy trade fired. Validation and pseudo-holdout remain
  sealed; this ML branch is stopped. Review:
  `docs/reviews/2026-08-02-low-tf-l2-expected-return.md`.
- The current build and July 31 classifier sidecar have identical 56,130 OOS
  signal/side identities and identical entry, exit, status, resolution time,
  and gross return on every row. The source-frame book hash matches, but bars
  and trades hashes differ. Treat V2 as current-input evidence, not a claim of
  byte-identical historical features; the older sidecar lacks the feature
  columns needed to identify whether dtype normalization or source revision
  caused the hash change.
- V1 used an L1 LightGBM objective and therefore estimated the conditional
  median rather than expected return. It remains immutable failed evidence.
  V2 corrects the contract to conditional-mean regression.
- Historical v1 note: the profile formerly separated all-pair observation from
  a single `range_onset_bidirectional_v1|SOL` execution mapping. The newer v2
  section at the top of this handoff supersedes that authority scope.
- Fresh intents outside that scope are retained with
  `provider_symbol_observe_only`. The live audit records both scope sets and
  fails if any action appears outside the configuration identity. The rolling
  checkpoint inherits this validation.
- Screen 4 projects exact backend scope and retains
  `EXECUTION SCOPE UNAVAILABLE` for older ledgers. TUI navigation accepts keys
  1-4, Tab, and direct mouse clicks after restart.
- Verification: 2,711 non-network Python tests passed with four intentional
  deselections and 27 existing deprecation warnings; all 108 Rust/TUI tests
  passed; strict Clippy, Rust formatting, changed-file Ruff, status, and diff
  whitespace checks passed. No live command was armed and no order was placed.
